# BACKLOG.md — OTV5 — v5.34

**This tree's backlog.** Same rules as `WORKING_AGREEMENT.md` §18: ships in every
archive, one row per item, a title bump and a PART 3 entry per delivery. States (the
LAST cell, which `tests/check_ledger_parity.py` reads): 🔲 OPEN · ◐ built/pushed, not
yet proven on a box · ✅ closed · ⬛ dead. Revisions of THIS tree are cited bare
inside this repo (`r3`) and by prefix outside it (`OTV5 r3`); revisions of any other
repository are always prefixed (`OTV4TEST r316`, `dtp r489`) — a bare number here is
a row in `docs/GENESIS.md`, which starts at r0.

**PART 1 is every open and unproven item, filed by category (r22).** It holds the seed's inherited rows
(OTV4TEST, their citations prefixed) and, since 2026-10-25, every open row of mainline's backlog (otv4) - the
operator: "Migrate the open otv4 backlog items into the otv5 backlog". Rows are verbatim except: a moved row
says so and carries otv4's status cell, its bare citations prefixed `otv4`; a moved id already used here took
the next free number and names its otv4 id; an id that named two open rows was split, the older taking the
next free number. Rows closed in place moved to PART 2. Within a category the inherited rows come first, newest
first, then the moved ones.

**BUILT ≠ PUSHED ≠ BAKED** — the third means running on a box with the operator
watching the plan ledger daily.

---

| **BAR.1** | ◐ (r44 BUILT; 10-28 read: right by time, but at 0-2 s past the boundary the just-ended row is provisional - fixed by BAR.2 r51; proof owed: the next entry that fires in the first seconds of a minute logs a signal bar, stop and close that belong to the bar that has just closed, not the one before it) 🔴 **"THE LAST CLOSED BAR" WAS A POSITION, SO IN THE FIRST INSTANT OF A MINUTE IT WAS A MINUTE STALE.** Every strategy, engine and exit took the last closed 1m bar to be df_1m.iloc[-2], on the assumption that the frame's newest row is the minute still forming. On a tick before the new minute's first row exists, the newest row IS the bar that has just closed. META 2026-10-27 (3f2b1372): a Breakout short fired at 10:10:00 ET on the 10:08 bar (close 623.01, under the 623.20 opening-range low; the plan line's "1m stop 623.57" is 10:08's high 623.5659). The 10:09 bar (O 623.01 H 623.56 L 623.01 C 623.21) had already closed back inside, so the bot fired on a break the market had undone. This breaks breakout_plan's "THE BREAK: a CLOSED bar's CLOSE beyond the edge (r5)" and the fresh-break rule. Of the four other 10-26/10-27 entries that fired in the first 2 s of a minute, SOFI 87de220f (LiquidityHunt 10-26 09:40:00) is proven to have read the stale bar (09:38 for 09:39; both above the range). For NVDA 3408e697, CRM b4e63251 and SPX 3a164066 the log cannot tell which bar was read. In all four cases both candidate bars give the same decision. The fix is utils/time_utils v5.1 closed_pos / closed_part: a row has closed when its start plus the bar length is at or before now. A frame whose newest row started 3 or more bar lengths ago, or one that cannot be read, keeps the old reading. Every last-closed read now uses it, in 11 files: breakout_plan, runaway_plan, liquidity_hunt, tcs_plan, condor_roll, atp_butterfly_plan, volt_plan, orb_engine (pinned once per update; the replay is unchanged), exit_engine (VOLT's 5m frame uses bar_s 300), derived/levels and main. Gate tests/check_last_closed_bar.py C1-C4. Operator: "Yes, do 1-4". | ◐ |

By exit class (both sides of the trade): trail +$3,569 (exit side +$2,289), stop -$2,454.50, target -$617 (exit side -$885), hard_close +$283 (3 of 84 trades). By strategy: Runaway 134 of 593 trades, -$841.50; ORB 57/375, +$473.50; Breakout 6/28, +$1,754 ($1,560 of it one trade); Sweep 9/157, -$769.50; Trend 12/61, +$175; GEXPin 3/91, -$38. 🔑 **THE CAUSE, IN THE CODE (OTV5 7c44791):** the chain is fetched ONCE at the top of the tick (main.py:6373, handed to run_analysis at main.py:6378, ctx["chain"] at main.py:2243) and nothing re-reads a quote before a fill is booked. EXITS: main.py:6503 manage_open_position(chain=ctx chain) -> position_manager.py:1000 _fetch_current_premium(record, chain) -> position_manager.py:1405-1406 place_exit_order(mark_price=current_premium) -> **exit_engine.py:3400-3404** books FillResult(fill_price=mark_price). ENTRIES: main.py:4383 reuses the same ctx chain -> the strategy stamps entry_premium=c.mark (e.g. strategy/breakout_plan.py:667) -> **entry_engine.py:989** paper_fill_price(signal.entry_premium) (butterfly entry_engine.py:995 on net_debit, standing offer entry_engine.py:743, credit verticals main.py:2988 net_credit = signal.net_credit). The mark is that snapshot's mid (data/options_chain.py:671), read from chain_marks rows the feed flushes every 2 s (data/candle_feed.py:338) and accepted up to OT_CHAIN_STALE_S = 120 s old (data/options_chain.py:154, 612); PAPER_FILL_SLIPPAGE_PCT is 0.0 (config.py:2721). The row is stamped with the booking time, the price with the fetch time. LIVE is touched too: the live single-leg limit anchors on the same snapshot mid (entry_engine.py:610). **DECISION NEEDED (the operator's), BEFORE CASH, NOT DURING THE FREEZE:** a cash fill is the broker's, so this is a paper measurement error, but every paper EV figure - the 10-26..11-20 benchmark included - carries it, and the live limit starts from the same stale mid. Under the trading-code freeze paper keeps booking as today; the daily review can re-book at the nearest edge, read-only, from this audit's method. A fix, if ruled, lands before SOFI's cash start (2026-11-23): book paper fills on a quote read at fill time, or refuse a fill whose quote is older than N s. | 🔲 MEASURED, NOT RULED |

STUDY (same file): a 5 s mark watch beat as-run by ~$6.8k on 85 QQQ-TEST stop exits (36%) - an UPPER bound (mid fills, zero latency); mainline's live form is otv4 EXIT.5. execution/fill_observers.observe_watch: main's end-of-tick sleep runs in 2 s slices (the SAME total) and records to data/counterfactual/quote_watch.jsonl when an open position's own chain_marks quote, at most 4 s old, is at or below watched_stop (trail if armed, else stop_premium; NOT the Hunt's held floor, VOLT, ORB, a fly/spread, or a resting OTV4TEST r266 row); at most once per position per 10 s. Paper never sees it; SOFI is the first box it would bite. | **REST.1** | 🔴 **THE EXIT CROSSES THE SPREAD AND THE STANDING-OFFER MACHINERY TO STOP IT ALREADY EXISTS — ENTRY-SIDE ONLY.** Operator, 2026-09-21, on being market-closed into the bell: *"We're basically saying yeah we know you're gonna liquidate us. Here's our order."* And the refinement that makes it a spec: *"That's exactly right for a late entry like that. **The limit order should immediately follow the entry.**"* 📊 **MEASURED ON TODAY'S OWN FILL:** the par-delta exit (10x QQQ 734C) filled at **5.835 with parity at 5.93** — we sold **ten cents under intrinsic**, **$100**, not to a bad market but to our own market order. The same slippage on a 74-contract Breakout is **$740**. ⚠️ **AND FRC.1'S ARITHMETIC MAKES THIS THE HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM ON THE BOARD:** `resting_orders.py`'s own header records the fleet at **+$2.70/trade gross edge against $126/trade of round-trip friction**, with half the half-spread worth **~$31/trade** — *"an order of magnitude more than anything on the trade-selection list."* 🔑 **THE MECHANISM IS BUILT AND POINTED THE WRONG WAY.** `execution/resting_orders.py` v1.1 is *"THE ORB STANDING OFFER: one limit at mark, posted once, left to rest"* (operator, 2026-08-30) — **entry-side, ORB-only**. This is the same thing on the EXIT side, for every strategy. **THE DESIGN, from the operator's spec:** the resting sell limit is placed **immediately after the entry fill**, not at 15:40 and not when delta arrives — a 15:30 entry has fifteen minutes and cannot afford to wait for a queue. Priced at **`parity − ε` and NOTHING ELSE**, re-priced each tick. 🔴 **THE TARGET MUST NEVER ENTER THE PRICE, AND MY FIRST CUT OF THIS ROW GOT IT WRONG — the operator caught it: *"Is that essentially a 100% take profit or are we able to get multiples higher?"*** `max(target, parity − ε)` REINTRODUCES the +100% guillotine OTV4TEST r82 just removed: on today's VOLT the mark touches the 2.01 target when parity is 2.00, so the limit fills at **+101% for $1,010** against **+491% / $4,910** parity-only — **a $3,900 error on one trade**. ⚠️ `limit_ladder.py`'s own header states the principle that was violated: *"The exit TRIGGER decides WHEN to start closing. It NEVER anchors WHERE the limit sits. That separation is the whole point."* A target is a TRIGGER; putting it in the price is smuggling a trigger into an anchor. 🔑 **PARITY HAS NO CEILING** — it is `spot − strike`, so a 20-point move is a 20.00 limit that re-prices upward every tick and can only be lifted at fair value. This is **not a take-profit; it is a fair-value exit**: delta decides WHEN, parity decides AT WHAT PRICE. Below par delta the order does not rest at all, because parity is not fair value while real extrinsic remains. `limit_at_mark(mark, floor=...)` ALREADY DOES `px = max(px, floor)` — the floor parameter exists and no caller passes it. Every input is in hand: spot from `df_1m`, strike on the record, `current_delta` stashed by `position_manager`. ⚠️ **THE RISK IS ONE-SIDED, WHICH IS RARE ENOUGH TO STATE:** if the resting order fills you keep parity less a tick; if it does not, 15:45 crosses **exactly as it does today**. Worst case IS the current behaviour. ⚠️ **IT ALSO ARMS ~90 MINUTES EARLIER** than the 15:40 ladder — today's VOLT hit par delta at 14:11. 🔴 **CANNOT BE VALIDATED IN PAPER:** `resting_orders.py` v1.1 records the operator's own ruling *"in paper mode, they ALL fill"*, so queue position, partial fills and non-fills are unmodelled here — the same blindness as the expiry path (EXP.1). A live measurement is the only proof. | 🔲 OPEN — specified, not built |
| **EXT.1** | ✅ **PAR DELTA IS AN EXIT — AND NO TRAIL IN THIS TREE IS DELTA-AWARE.** Operator, live 2026-09-21: *"When Delta reaches PAR, we need to get the fuck out."* 🔑 **INTRINSIC IS EARNED; EXTRINSIC IS WHAT YOU ARE STILL PAYING FOR.** When extrinsic decays away the position is stock with an expiry: delta ~1.0, **no convexity left to absorb a pullback**, so a give-back expressed as a fraction of the run costs full dollars exactly when the trade has already won. Measured on 81b1afae: mark 5.63 / intrinsic 5.59 / delta 0.983 — **$45 of theta risk against $2,880 of leash on $4,630 held**; the identical leash on a delta-0.30 position costs ~$860 because curvature eats the move. Shipped for **VOLT only** at OTV4TEST r79. 🔴 **THE GENERAL GAP IS STILL OPEN:** ORB, Breakout, sweep and the FVG trail are all calibrated in run-fraction or premium-% terms and **none reads delta**. Only two rules in the tree read it at all — `_velocity_stall` (observe-only, `VELOCITY_STALL_ENFORCE=0`, scoped to `("ORBStrategy",)`, **zero log lines ever**) and `_theta_bleed` (deliberately mute above the trail-arm threshold, **zero exits all-time**) — and both use it only for break-even velocity, never to size a leash. ⚠️ **NOT GENERALISED ON PURPOSE:** Breakout's exits are ruled unchanged (EXIT.3) and every other strategy is unmeasured. Arm E of `PREREG_TRAIL.md` measures the delta-scaled leash before it spreads. ◐ OTV4TEST r79 — VOLT only; the fleet-wide gap is OPEN | ◐ OTV4TEST r79 |

| **EXP.1** | ⬜ **THE EXPIRY PATH IS UNMODELLED AND THE ACCOUNTING WOULD BE WRONG IF IT EVER RAN.** Measured 2026-09-21 over the whole book: **69 closed trades, ZERO ever exited at or after 15:45 ET**, and **no exit reason anywhere mentions expiry, exercise, assignment or settlement**. `hard_close_15:45` has fired **once, all-time**. 🔑 **QQQ IS PHYSICALLY SETTLED AND AMERICAN-STYLE**, so a long ITM 0DTE does not lapse, it **exercises into stock**: today's 74-contract Breakout would have delivered **7,400 shares / $5,453,800** against **$4,144** of premium — **1,316x**. ⚠️ **AND CHEAPNESS MAKES IT WORSE, WHICH INVERTS THE INSTINCT:** the same $1,050 buys 210 contracts at $0.05 (21,000 shares) or 10 at $1.00 (1,000 shares) — **21x the delivery exposure for identical premium risk**. The nastiest case is not the loss but the FLAT one: closing $0.01 ITM books −$840 *and still delivers $15.6M of stock*. 🔴 **THE BOOKING IS WRONG:** `broker_reconcile.match_closing_fills` returns None when no closing order exists — *"expiry/assignment leave no closing order"* — and the caller *"falls back to the flagged $0.00"*, so a position worth intrinsic would book as a **total loss**. ✅ **THE DECISION PATH IS SOUND, THOUGH, AND WAS DRIVEN TO PROVE IT:** par delta (OTV4TEST r82) fires at **15:39**, before the flatten window opens, and `hard_close` returns exit=True at 15:41, 15:46, 15:59 and 16:01. The residual risk is the **FILL**, not the decision. ⚠️ `PAPER never calls this — the DB is truth there`, so none of it has ever executed on this box. Related: [[REST.1]] is the fill-side fix; buying the next expiry after 12:00 deletes the class entirely but surrenders the gamma and the contract count. ◐ (OTV4TEST r165 BUILT) — ACCOUNTING FIXED: an expired position books its 16:00 settlement value (boot Step 1 `close_expired_open_trades(settle=)` and the phantom path), shares in the account page (`send_exercise_footprint_alert`), and the v4-port `exit_price` column bug in `close_phantom` is fixed (a live recovered fill would have raised). Measured 09-27: 0 exits at/after 15:55 in 182 trades here and 0 past 16:00 in 795 fleet trades (1-REPORTER, Q-EXP1-0927); the only near-close strandings fleet-wide were deliberate bakes, recovered. The emergency watchdog (operator: *"Not a bad idea to have one for 'emergencies'"*) is BUILT at OTV4TEST r168 - row WDOG.1; tastytrade's auto-exercise/DNE rules are the operator's to confirm; no late-restart guard (operator: *"I don't need that"*). | ◐ OTV4TEST r165 |

| **PRE.6** | (id was PRE.5 until the 2026-10-03 cleanup - it named two rows) 🔲 **THE WAREHOUSE'S CANDLE SERIES ABOVE 1m ARE SYSTEMATICALLY PARTIAL — A MAINLINE DEFECT, FOR THE CONTROL AGENT, NOT THIS BOX.** Found 2026-09-18 while measuring the zone width on SPX and MU. **`warehouse/s3_push.py:886`**: `SELECT * FROM candles WHERE symbol=? AND interval=? AND ts_epoch_ms > ?` — `push_candles` advances a **high-water mark on the BAR'S OWN TIMESTAMP**. Once a bar is pushed its stamp sits at or below the watermark **forever**, so every later `UPDATE` to that same row — which is what a 1h bar *is* for the 59 minutes after its first tick — **is never pushed again.** Whichever state the pusher happened to catch is what the warehouse keeps. ⚠️ **CORRECT FOR 1m, WRONG FOR EVERY LONGER INTERVAL:** a 1m bar is written once; 5m, 15m, 1h and 1d are mutable rows. 🔑 **AND THE SAME FILE ALREADY HAS THE RIGHT PATTERN** — `push_trades` at `:613` pushes *"every trade row whose CONTENT has changed since last push"*, content-based CDC. `push_candles` uses a timestamp watermark, and for a mutable row those are not the same thing. **MEASURED, every 1h object in `raw/candles` across 31 partitions:** SPX has **ZERO complete bars since 2026-08-21** — median push lag **−59.5 minutes**, i.e. every bar pushed ~30s after its hour OPENED and never again, four weeks of opening ticks; QQQ is partial but survivable at 11–16 complete of 28–44 rows per bar-date, so its coverage is thin rather than absent. ⚠️ **MEASURED-ON-ONE-BOX-ONLY, AND THE CONTROL AGENT'S INDEPENDENT READ DISAGREES (OTV4TEST r65).** It measured **QQQ 0 of 21 complete** on 2026-09-16/17/18 — every symbol it sampled fully frozen, not SPX-shaped at all. Ours may have come from an earlier window. **The discrepancy was deliberately NOT bought**: both sides agreed it changes no conclusion — recoverability is settled per-interval by the fifteen-box fan-out and the 08-21 start date is confirmed independently on both sides — and a dedicated S3 run is the ~3,700 sequential `get_object` sweep §38.1 warns about. **The honest state of this figure is one box, one window, not a fleet property.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ABOUT SPX IS BROKEN** — its box pushes 192 objects a day, trades/EOD/plan_ledger all current through 09-17. The defect simply lands on its 16-rows-a-day cadence every time. ⚠️ **THE SILENCE IS THE COST:** any control-side report reading warehouse candles above 1m has been reading opening ticks some invisible fraction of the time, and it renders as a slightly-wrong number, never an error — the plausible-silence class this repo keeps finding in its own code. **OWED: a heads-up to the control agent with the evidence** (`s3_push.py:886`, the −59.5min SPX lag since 08-21, the `push_trades` contrast at `:613`). **NOT OURS TO FIX** — the brief is explicit that mainline is only touched for a significant flaw found here, and this is the "surface it" half, not the "change it" half. **Operator's ruling, 2026-09-18: address it on a weekend.** ⚠️ **AND IT NARROWS A CLAIM I MADE THE SAME NIGHT:** the zone-width replication on SPX rests on July–August backfills only, so the width is replicated on QQQ and MU cleanly and on SPX's older data — a smaller sample than I first presented. 🔑 **DISCHARGED 2026-09-19: DELIVERED TO THE CONTROL AGENT, AND IT CAME BACK STRONGER.** It verified the mechanism independently in otv4 r389 **by reading** (`s3_push.py:926` the hwm load, `:929` the bound SELECT, `:935`→`:945` the watermark advance) and found a better contrast than ours: **`push_trades:636`'s own docstring states the reasoning `push_candles` needed and did not get** — *"Trade rows MUTATE — written at entry, rewritten at exit"* — one function above the one that needed it. **That is what moves this from a design trade-off to a defect.** 🔑 **THEIR SCOPE NOTE, CREDITED, AND IT RESCUED ONE OF OUR OWN RESULTS:** `raw/candles` and `raw/ohlc` are **different pushers** — the file-based one at `:871` keys its ledger on a **content sha**, so the `ohlc` prefix is **UNAFFECTED**. **CONSEQUENCE: OTV4TEST r47 STANDS.** Our volume-expansion study (n=736, back to 2026-07-08) read `ohlc`, and it is load-bearing — it is the measurement that ruled volume expansion does not separate a breakout, and therefore why the Breakout's gates are a router rather than a filter. Had it been built on watermark-frozen bars the whole gate design reopened. **It was not, and we would not have known to check without their paragraph.** 🔴 **THE REPAIR IS NOT FORWARD-ONLY, AND THERE IS A DEADLINE.** Their first reading was that a CDC fix could not heal the frozen bars because the store is a pruned rolling window (`push_candles`' own docstring). **Measured here instead** — `RETENTION_DAYS` at `warehouse/retention_purge.py:172`, against `feed_store.db` depth on 2026-09-19: `1m` floor 09-14 (policy 5d, exact) · `5m` floor **09-09** (policy 10d, exact) · `15m` floor **09-02** (policy 20d, **SHALLOWER**) · `1h` floor **07-21** (policy 60d, exact) · `1d` floor **08-10** (never purged). **1h reaches a month BEFORE the 08-21 corruption, so the rows that would supply the correct values were never pruned** — a CDC fix or a one-time watermark reset above 1m heals the existing range, not merely future bars. **CLOCK: the 08-21 bars age out of the 60-day window around 2026-10-20**, rolling forward daily after that. 5m (08-21→09-09) and 15m (08-21→09-02) are **already partly gone**. 🔑 **TWO GENERAL RULES THIS PRODUCED, AND THEY OUTLIVE THE DEFECT.** (1) **`NEVER_PURGE` is a statement about the purge, not about the data** — our `1d` series has no purge clock and still has a floor, at 08-10, because that is when collection began; on a box whose daily collection started after 08-21 there is nothing to recover and the policy would never say so. (2) **The policy is a ceiling on depth, not a promise of it** — `15m` measured three days shallower than its own window. **Every row of a recoverability table must be measured per box per interval or it is an upper bound wearing a fact's clothes.** ⚠️ **THEIR PARTIAL-REPAIR WARNING, WHICH IS THE BEST POINT EITHER SIDE MADE:** a watermark reset on `5m` would re-push correct rows for the last ten days and **nothing** for 08-21→09-09, leaving the bucket holding a mix of healed and frozen bars **with nothing distinguishing them** — worse than uniformly bad data, because it destroys the one property telling a reader which rows to distrust. **And the marking must be computed BY the repair, not appended after it**: an operation that knows which rows it could not heal already holds everything the marker needs, and a second pass is the thing that does not happen. **The marker is per symbol per interval**, carrying that series' own floor. ⚠️ **CONSTRAINT ON OUR OWN RESEARCH SURFACE, recorded because it binds every future study:** anything we read from `raw/candles` **above 1m** over the affected window is reading frozen opening ticks. `raw/candles/interval=1m` is safe; `raw/ohlc` is safe; everything else above 1m carries the caveat until the bucket is repaired or marked. **We do not push, so we contribute nothing to the corruption — we only read it**, which is the asymmetry that explains why this fork found it and mainline did not. **STILL OWED TO US:** their fifteen-box `SELECT interval, MIN(ts_epoch_ms), COUNT(*) FROM candles GROUP BY interval;` fan-out, **read 1d-floor-first** — a 1h gap announces itself as a deadline, a 1d floor after 08-21 announces nothing at all. Blocked on their side by a dead Bash tool, not by a decision. ◐ heads-up DELIVERED 2026-09-19 — the repair is mainline's | 🔲 OPEN |

We may want to consider a 10-contract minimum when selecting strikes"* and *"that's worth exploring & if we can benefit from it, I'm not opposed."* 📊 **THE CURVE, FROM OUR OWN MODEL — the one [[FEE.3]] reconciled line-by-line against a real statement:** QQQ single-leg round trip at \$1.00 premium costs **\$1.12/contract up to 10 lots and \$0.12/contract MARGINAL beyond it** — a **9.3x drop at the knee**, because the \$10/leg commission cap binds at exactly ten. Our fleet's average trade on 2026-09-21 was **7.2 contracts**: full per-contract freight, zero cap benefit. 🔴 **§0.1 CORRECTION, MINE, THE SAME EVENING — I OVER-WEIGHTED THIS BEFORE THE OPERATOR COULD ACT ON IT.** I reported fees as *"42% of gross"*, which is true and misleading: it is **a ratio with a small numerator**. Measured against deployed premium instead — gross **3.39%**, fees **1.41%**, net **1.98%** of \$28,613 — so recovering the knee is worth about **80bps of premium**, taking net from 1.98% to roughly 2.8%. **Real (about 40% more money for identical trades) and NOT the order-of-magnitude term.** That term is DEPLOYMENT: on QQQ, same budget and same day, we put **\$3,083** of premium to work against the test repo's **~\$58,000** (3 trades against 26), while our **return per dollar was 18.2% against their 16.5%**. A count is not a finding until its unit is stated — [[C.39]], and the second time in two days I have quoted the wrong denominator ([[OPS.22]]). ⚠️ **SPX IS EXEMPT AND IT IS THE BIGGEST SYMBOL WE HAVE.** The \$10 cap does NOT apply to broad-based index options; SPX carries a **\$0.60/contract exchange fee on BOTH sides** and never caps ([[FEE.2]]). So a ten-lot minimum buys **nothing** on the name that netted **+\$13,528** over the banked window — it covers 14 of 15. 🔴 **AND THE MECHANISM DECIDES WHETHER THIS IS SAFE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FILED RATHER THAN BUILT. THREE OPTIONS, NOT ONE:** **(1) REFUSE anything that sizes below 10.** Honest and precedented — §36's **FEASIBILITY** class exists for vetoes on trades that cannot pay however good they look. Costs us trades, and the count is measurable from the banked corpus before anything ships. **(2) PICK A CHEAPER STRIKE until the budget affords 10.** 🔴 **THIS IS THE DANGEROUS ONE AND IT LOOKS LIKE THE CHEAP ONE.** Cheaper means further OTM, and **strike already encodes conviction** — [[MOM.1]] in our own words: *"a barely-qualifying signal takes a near-the-money strike and so participates in a small move; the contract selection itself refuses to reward a weak read."* Forcing size by going cheaper **inverts that**, systematically pushing every marginal setup further out, lowering delta and raising the odds of total loss, **while rendering as a fee optimisation**. §36's line exactly: not a looser version of the same trade, **a different trade**. **(3) RAISE THE PER-BOX BUDGET** so ten lots is affordable at the CORRECT strike. Pure risk decision, and [[C.33]] is the bound — fifteen boxes, ONE brokerage account, no coordination, so 15x the budget is the real exposure. 🔑 **AND WHEREVER IT LANDS, IT LIVES IN `RiskManager.size_for` AND NOWHERE ELSE** — [[C.22]]: *every size must come from `size_for()` and arrive in `SizingResult.contracts`*, because otv4 r181's rule lived in `_execute_entry_signal`, wrote `signal.contracts`, and was **written 4x and read 0x tree-wide** — a fleet-wide 1-lot for two days. A fee-aware minimum applied in a caller is inert in exactly the same way and would look like it shipped. ⚠️ **THE SAME GROUND HAS BEEN LITIGATED ONCE:** [[C.24]] gave ORB a cap exemption so a setup the budget refused still traded at ≥1 lot, and **[[C.32]] REVERSED it** — ruled on RISK APPETITE (*"I'm actually good, even with the worst case"*) which **is not the same question as ACCOUNT CAPACITY**. A minimum is that argument from the other end and should not be re-made from scratch. **WHAT SATURDAY MEASURES BEFORE ANY OPTION IS CHOSEN:** how many banked trades fall in the 5-9 dead zone, what the drag has cost in dollars, what option (1) would have refused and what those trades did, and whether the knee survives at real premiums rather than the \$1.00 used to draw the curve. **§38.8 holds it to a weekend and the choice is the operator's, not a fee calculation.** 📊 **THE BASELINE THIS IS MEASURED AGAINST, BECAUSE THE ROW IS USELESS WITHOUT IT — AND THE REPO IS PROFITABLE.** Operator, 2026-09-21: *"this repo IS profitable over the time we've been trading this setup."* **Measured over the ENGINE-EPOCH window (2026-09-01 onward, [[S3.23]]'s floor), 14 sessions, 588 closed trades, 15 boxes: gross \$14,566.50, modelled fees \$7,980.24, NET AFTER FEES +\$6,586.26** — about \$470 a session. 🔴 **AND THAT IS WHERE THE FEE FINDING GETS ITS REAL SIZE: FEES ARE 54.8% OF GROSS OVER THE WINDOW, NOT THE 42% A SINGLE GOOD DAY SHOWED.** They are the largest single deduction this book takes. ⚠️ **THE CEILING IS NOT THE WHOLE \$7,980 AND MUST BE DECOMPOSED BEFORE ANYONE QUOTES A NUMBER:** only the **\$1.00/contract commission** caps; the exchange and regulatory components are per-contract on every lot and do not, SPX never caps at all, and sizing above the knee is a RISK decision under [[C.33]] rather than a free lunch. Saturday's first job is that split — how much of the \$7,980 is CAPPABLE commission — because the honest headline is *"we could have recovered \$X of \$7,980"* and nobody has computed X. ⚠️ **AND 588 TRADES OVER 14 SESSIONS IS THE SAMPLE ANY MERGE COMPARISON OWES ITS OTHER SIDE** (§12): a single box's single session, with 50.6% of its P&L in one fill, answers a different question. |
| **FEE.1** | 🔴 **EVERY P&L NUMBER THIS SYSTEM HAS EVER PRINTED IS GROSS.** (moved from otv4 BACKLOG 2026-10-25; otv4 status: otv4 r293)  | 🔲 **STAYS — fees are CALCULATED and REPORTED, never inside EV** (the operator, 2026-10-24: "I want fees calculated, but not in our EV due to differences in contract quantities that will be addressed later"). ◐ **BUILT — not yet wired to any report.** Read at source, not assumed: `exit_engine` computes `pnl_usd = (current_premium - entry_prem) * contracts * CONTRACT_MULTIPLIER` at all eight sites and `position_manager:655` does the same. A grep for *fee* and *commission* across every `.py` in both repos returns only the word *feed*. New `tests/fees.py` v1.0 — control-only, pure, imports nothing that trades — computes per-trade fees from the operator's tastytrade card (2026-07-30). ⚠️ **CLASSIFICATION DEFERS TO `strategy.structure.of()`**, the engine's own, never a strategy-name list (§otv4 r35) and never a second copy (C.23). ⚠️ **ABSENCE IS NEVER ZERO:** an unpriceable row returns `Unpriced` with a NAMED reason and `total_fees_usd` reports `unpriced` alongside the sum, always, including when it is zero. 21 checks, six mutations proven red. |
| **FEE.5** | ⬜ **ASSIGNMENT COSTS $5.00 PER EVENT AND NOTHING IN `trades` RECORDS THAT ONE HAPPENED.** (moved from otv4 BACKLOG 2026-10-25; otv4 status: otv4 r293)  | 🔲 **STAYS — fees are CALCULATED and REPORTED, never inside EV** (the operator, 2026-10-24: "I want fees calculated, but not in our EV due to differences in contract quantities that will be addressed later"). ⬜ **MEASURED, EXPOSED, DELIBERATELY NOT CHARGED.** Both A/E lines on the May statement moved **30 contracts and were charged $5.00 exactly** — per EVENT, not per contract ($150 would be per-contract). **And expiring worthless is FREE:** two EXPIRED lines, 30 and -30 contracts, no charge. 🔑 **THAT DISTINCTION IS LIVE FOR THE CREDIT BOOK:** otv4 r105 rules that the credit hard close *"takes the nickel or takes ASSIGNMENT"*, and those two dispositions cost **$0.00 and $5.00**, not the same. `ASSIGNMENT_FEE_PER_EVENT` and `EXPIRY_FEE` are exposed as constants and **`fees_for()` does not apply them** — F13b pins that — because no column distinguishes a closed trade from an assigned one, and charging it would be **inventing an event**. Needs an exit_reason or a column before it can be modelled. |

| **BOX.10** | 🔲 **NOTHING PAGES WHEN THE HALT FAILS, AND THE BOX BILLS UNTIL THE NEXT WAKE.** OTV4TEST r67 made `midnight_halt` read the result of `sudo shutdown -h now` — it was captured and **discarded**, with `main()` returning 0 unconditionally, so a refused shutdown logged *"halting"* and the unit **Finished successfully**. It now logs at ERROR and exits non-zero, so systemd marks the unit **failed**. ⚠️ **THAT IS VISIBILITY, NOT AN ALARM — NOTHING SENDS ANYTHING.** ⚠️ **AND THE OBVIOUS ALARM IS THE WRONG ONE:** *"no midnight message means something is wrong"* would page the operator every night he shuts down early, which he does — an alarm firing on an expected condition is one that stops being read (§17), so the alert must stay strictly event-driven and **the silence must never be built on**. ⚠️ **HONEST LIMIT ON THE RETURN CODE:** `shutdown -h now` returns 0 as soon as it SCHEDULES the halt, so a non-zero code is definite proof of failure (the sudo/permission class, the realistic one) but a **zero is not proof of success**. Proving the box actually went down can only be done from somewhere that is not the box. Needs a ruling on what, if anything, should page. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **MBX.1** | 🔲 **A SHARED S3 MAILBOX BETWEEN THIS FORK AND THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT — DESIGNED 2026-09-19, NOT APPROVED, NOT BUILT, NOTHING EXISTS.** Proposed at the **mainline operator's instruction**; it needs **both** operators' sign-off and an IAM change, and neither agent requests the grant for the other. **SHAPE:** prefix `mailbox/` — deliberately **outside `raw/`**, which has a standing never-delete rule and must not acquire a second purpose. Layout `mailbox/to=<recipient>/from=<sender>/<sent_utc>-<msg_id>.json`, acks at `mailbox/ack/to=<original_sender>/<msg_id>.json` so each side lists exactly ONE prefix for its own receipts. Envelope mirrors the warehouse `_wrap` idiom plus `in_reply_to`. **The mailbox is TRANSPORT, not an archive** — 90-day lifecycle, and **anything that matters is landed into the RECEIVING side's own ledger by the receiving side**, because the record must outlive the lifecycle. 🔑 **OUR OBJECTIONS, ALL ACCEPTED BY THE OTHER SIDE.** (1) 🔴 **IT SOLVES DURABILITY AND AUDIT BUT NOT LATENCY, AND LATENCY IS WHERE 2026-09-19's ENTIRE VALUE CAME FROM** — a defect was surfaced and verified across four files in twenty minutes, and a wrong inference corrected in one round; through a polled mailbox both are days apart or never, against a clock expiring 10-20. **Nothing polls a mailbox**, and §38.7 says plainly that an assistant does not run continuously. So a **session-start LIST of one's own inbox prefix is a REQUIREMENT**, in the handoff reading order beside the transcript read — one LIST, no timer, no grant beyond the read. (2) **Both channels, different jobs** — the live session route for work where latency IS the value, the mailbox for durable handoff and session-death survival; a replacement would trade the thing that worked for the things that failed. (3) **`provenance` mandatory, and PER-CLAIM** — `measured` / `read` / `reported` / `inferred`, with the envelope pinned to the **weakest value present** as a backstop. The day produced two retractions and both were **an inference sent in the register of a measurement**; and `reported` is ours, because a third-party fact relayed onward is none of the other three and unlabelled hearsay is how a number launders itself into a permanent record. (4) **IAM tightenings:** Put scoped to the **other** side's inbox only (neither side can write its own inbox or overwrite the other's sent objects, so forged inbound is impossible), **no `DeleteObject` for either agent at all** (an agent that can delete can erase the audit trail), and an **explicit Deny on `s3:PutObject` for `raw/*`**, because our read grant is bucket-wide and an Allow scoped to `mailbox/*` is worth only as much as the absence of a second Allow nobody has audited. 🔴 **WHAT IT WOULD COST US, AND IT IS THE OPERATOR'S RULING AND NOT THE ASSISTANT'S.** It needs `s3:PutObject` on `vertigo-warehouse-tx9ai`. **Our OTV4TEST r31 grant is READ-ONLY and that is recorded as a SAFETY RULING, not a permission detail** (§38.3): this box does not push **because another QQQ box already does**, and a second pusher into `raw/.../sym=QQQ/` would not error — latest-wins by `pushed_at_utc` would silently overwrite the live box's rows in every fleet report. ⚠️ **That reason does not apply to a `mailbox/` prefix — there is no collision there — but the ruling as written is broader than the reason that produced it, and narrowing a safety ruling is the operator's call.** Nothing here implies unmasking `s3-push.service`, and the assistant would object if it did. **If the operator declines, the existing session channel plus the ACK convention is a working answer and the other side has been told so in those terms.** ◐ OTV4TEST r65 — **OUR OPERATOR HAS RULED: WANTED, DEFERRED.** 2026-09-19, verbatim: *"I want the mailbox, however this is working well for the moment. Perhaps during RTH we would tackle something like that."* **Not a decline and not a yes-now.** The live session channel proved sufficient across a full day of defect exchange, which is itself evidence for the original objection — the mailbox solves durability and audit, and latency is where the value turned out to be. Nothing built, nothing in the bucket, no IAM requested. His telemetry precondition stands unchanged whenever it is built. **Mainline's operator has not ruled; their agent has been told ours has, and that the ruling is "yes, later".** Revisit during RTH. | 🔲 OPEN |

The purge is bounded (measured, `purge(apply=True)` on scratch stores): one halt deletes nothing from that day - the shortest window is 3 days, series are push-mark clamped, candles and the chain_snapshots tree are age-only - so per event this is an ALARM defect, and a data-loss defect only across multi-day silent halts. **THE FIX, BOTH HALVES SHARED WITH otv4 (WA §38.2):** `warehouse/counter_pop.py` (otv4 r436 verbatim) removes ONLY ledger entries whose prefix LISTs zero objects, refuses partials and absent keys by name, takes the push lock (rc=3 without it), dry unless `--apply`; its gate `tests/check_counter_pop.py` v1.1 (otv4 r438 verbatim: N6 runs the WRITE path, which v1.0 never did - all four peer-run mutants were green on v1.0; N7 binds every name and call shape of the REAL s3_push, compiled from SOURCE because a stale good .pyc made the first N7 pass a same-size rename - reproduced here). `warehouse/self_close.py` v1.5 (2a): drift counts only when the DRAIN line reads `failed=0`; gate `tests/check_self_close_hold.py` H1-H5 + H4b, BORN RED on 0990101 at H4 and H4b only, three mutants killed. Operator 2026-09-26: fix 1 *'Concur'*, 2a approved, sequence *'Concur, and work closely with the peer'*. **NOT IN OTV4TEST r147, IN SEQUENCE:** 2b (an emptied prefix is reported EMPTIED, never drift) and ~~2c (heal only gaps <= 2)~~ **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-26 (operator: "Approve on all"), and WHY, so it is not proposed again:** it rested on s3_push's "a gap of 3+ STILL HOLDS THE BOX" comment, which PREDATES OTV4TEST r180 and was never updated — both agents read a stale comment as the specification. OTV4TEST r180 (2026-08-28, mainline GENESIS) heals any short prefix when the drain is clean (failed=0) and S3 holds objects (n>0), with NO gap bound, deliberately: the ledger counts PUTs while S3 counts KEYS, and re-pushes (timer, harvest, restart) inflate it past 2 as the steady state — the `<=2` in OTV4TEST r180's row is its PROBLEM STATEMENT ("the drift diagnosis only forgave gaps <=2 and two months of accumulation exceeded it"), not its remedy. | **RPT.21** | 🔴 **`exit_replay` WAS OOM-KILLED — IT LOADED THE WHOLE WINDOW'S QUOTES BEFORE REPLAYING ONE TRADE.** (moved from otv4 BACKLOG 2026-10-25; otv4 status: otv4 r326, otv4 r328)  | ◐ **BUILT.** Operator ran `Exit replay` over all history on 2026-09-09 and the menu printed `43702 Killed` partway through `quote_series 2026-08-24: 1437 object(s)`. **Not a crash — the kernel.** 🔑 **CAUSE:** `run_s3` called `load_series("quote_series", dates)` for the ENTIRE window and `load_series` extends one list across every date, so ~70 sessions of the warehouse's highest-volume stream had to fit in control's RAM before a single path was rebuilt. ⚠️ **v1.1's own comment is half right and that is why it survived:** *"ONE LIST CALL, NOT ONE PER TRADE"* — a fetch per trade would indeed be the expensive path, but the fix chosen was the opposite extreme. **Per DATE preserves the batching argument exactly** (every trade on a date still shares one indexed load) and shrinks the window from ~70 sessions to one. 🔑 **AND A DATE WITH NO CLOSED TRADES NOW LOADS NO QUOTES AT ALL** — on this run that skipped 60+ sessions whose result was guaranteed to be nothing, including the pre-epoch dates otv4 r314 emptied. ⚠️ **THERE IS NO TRACEBACK IN THIS FAILURE AND NO PYTHON-LEVEL ERROR TO ASSERT ON**, which is why `--selftest` passed, pyflakes was clean, and a synthetic end-to-end run rendered fine — the property has to be checked AT THE CALL. **GATE:** NEW `tests/check_exit_replay_streams.py` drives `run_s3` against a stub `warehouse_source` (E1 one date per `load_series` · E2 trade-free dates load nothing · E3 every date with trades is replayed · E4 `render` draws from a filled accumulator · E5 `accumulate` adds rather than replaces). Born red at `a14ab00` on E1/E3/E4/E5, with E1 printing the exact whole-window call. `run()` split into `accumulate()` + `render()`; the `--db` path and the selftest are unchanged. 🔴 **AND IT WAS STILL KILLED AFTER otv4 r326 — ON ONE DATE (otv4 r328).** Per-date was the right direction and not far enough: `quote_series` is a per-tick stream over ~250 chain symbols, so a single session does not fit control's memory either and narrowing again only moves the wall. **THE LIST WAS THE PROBLEM, NOT THE WINDOW.** `warehouse_source.iter_series` (v1.4) streams envelopes instead of returning a list, and exit_replay now indexes **only the streamer symbols that date's trades actually name**, taken from `legs_of` — the same function the replay uses, never guessed. A day's quotes cover the whole chain; a day's trades touch a handful of contracts. ⚠️ Progress prints per symbol-day: the killed run printed ONE banner then went silent, the otv4 r298 rule unmet on this path. 🔑 **AND THE GATE WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE WRONG THING** — otv4 r326's E1 asserted one DATE per `load_series` call and PASSED against code that was still fatal; **v1.1** asserts streaming per symbol-day AND that `load_series` is never reached at all, born red at `9a09e49` on E1/E2. A gate can be correct about what it measures and wrong about what matters. |

one reproducing case plus a banked counterfactual; not a statistical claim. **(2) STRUCTURAL TRUTH** — established on the DEEP TAPE, `ohlc` **51 sessions back to 2026-07-08** and `chain_snapshots` 39 back to 07-24, via `tests/tape_harness.py` / `replay_confluence.py` and NOT a third lineage (§25's 774-line rebuild reintroduced the uncapped-frames bug). **(3) CALIBRATION** — ships as a SHADOW POLICY on OTV4TEST's `derived/counterfactual.py` pattern, recording what it WOULD have done beside the live rule, judged on paired real outcomes after weeks. ⚠️ **THE P&L CORPUS IS A RULING, NOT A DATA GAP:** dtp r314 severed pre-09-01 trades on the operator's instruction, so P&L is ~493 trades over 13 sessions growing ~140/week while tape and chains reach July; he reaffirmed the split 2026-09-16 — *"the trades from that period are probably not helpful… however the chains & tapes will help inform your studies."* The 8,313 stripped objects are SOFT-deleted and recoverable. ⬜ **DELIVERABLE:** two separately-approvable packages — DEFECTS and CALIBRATIONS — each change tagged by LAYER (plan vs strategy), each carrying its dollars, its sample, its counterfactual method AND that method's limits, and what would falsify it. **"No change this week" is a valid output** (§0.3). ⬜ **TARGET IS otv4 ONLY**; OTV4TEST is the operator's sandbox, read-only to me. ⬜ **SEQUENCE:** 09-19 manual run → `docs/SATURDAY_DEEPDIVE.md` written FROM it, because a timer-launched thread has no memory and rebuilds from the repo (§38.4) → a second run → **then the operator installs the timer**, `claude -p` non-interactive, same shape as `dtp-morning.timer`. ⚠️ **THE FIRST RUN'S OWN LESSON, ALREADY PAID:** 2026-09-16 produced a confident finding from a two-day subset that the full record reversed ([[ORB.16]]). Phase 0 of the procedure ranks losses across the WHOLE corpus before any hypothesis is stated. 🔴 **PHASE 0 ALSO SWEEPS BOTH TREES' CHECKERS, FIRST, BEFORE ANY ANALYSIS** — operator's instruction, 2026-09-18, and it exists because of [[CHK.6]]: a checker written on 09-16 went red on the next trading session and stayed red for three, and it was found **only because an unrelated delivery happened to sweep dtp.** ⚠️ **NOTHING SWEEPS EITHER TREE ON A SCHEDULE.** The lander runs the CHECK lines a spec happens to declare and `check_land_discipline`, `check_shell_parses` and the two maps — it does not run the suite, and a checker nobody names is a checker nobody runs (SHD.5's shape, and LAND.9's: `check_land_sh` sat dead for five days and 24 commits because no spec named it, and a dead gate hides the next check that rots inside it). So the weekly sweep is the routine that turns luck into schedule. ⚠️ **AND THE RED SET IS DIFFED, NOT EYEBALLED:** the deliverable is the set compared against the prior week's, because the four standing otv4 reds (`check_configure_relaxed`, `check_fill_basis`, `check_ledger_parity`, `check_wing_search`) make a raw count meaningless — what matters is a red that is NEW, and a green that used to be red. ⚠️ **A NEWLY-RED CHECKER IS PHASE 1's FIRST TARGET**, ahead of the loss table: it is a defect by construction (n=1 provable, §0.6's own class) and it may be the reason a loss went unnoticed. |

THE RULE: a CLOSED (span + 5 s) 5m/15m/1h bar whose every minute is held (plain: the 09:30-bell minutes, so the 09:00 hour needs 30 and half days count; *_EXT: the full span, from *_EXT 1m only) keeps whichever version is closer to its 1m aggregate (volume first, then high/low; 0-volume: high/low then open/close; a tie keeps the stored row). Forming bars, incomplete minutes, stored NULLs and any error take FEED.9b with NO extra read on a forming bar (1-REPORTER's cost point). 1m and 1d are never refereed. OPEN LIMITATION, NAMED: the 1m set is the same vendor's, guarded only by FEED.9/9b, and completeness is by COUNT - a 1m slice (thin minutes) passes as complete and would mislead the referee. | **EOD.1** | [2026-10-03 cleanup: BUILT AND RUNNING; kept open only because the SOFI/AAL bake named in its state cell was not confirmed.] 🔴 **THE OPERATOR'S END OF DAY (2026-09-26).** His words, in order: *"I wanna extend the debit window to all day. I want to extend the hard close for all trades to 1550. And for the TRADES that risk assignment I want to place resting limit orders at 1545"*; then *"1. Stop entries at 1540 / 2. Resting limit orders at 1545, ladder exits at 1550 if they're not the assignment risk type"*; the price *"Best case on the trajectory (nickel close, 1 delta, etc)"*; and *"Can we instead estimate their assumed BY 1555 & rest that?"*. ✅ **OTV4TEST r149 BUILT:** `config.EOD_SCHEDULE` = entries_stop 15:40 / resting_at 15:45 / ladder_at 15:50 / cross_at 15:55, and every end-of-day time derives from it. ORB, Runaway, Hunt, Breakout and VOLT run 09:35-15:40 (were 11:30); the debit cutoff is 15:40 and `OT_DEBIT_CUTOFF_ET` no longer moves it. `strategy.structure.has_assignment_risk` = any short leg (credit verticals, condor legs, tents, butterflies, adopted shorts). Assignment risk RESTS from 15:45 at its estimated value BY 15:55 if spot holds (Black-Scholes, r=0, 5 minutes to expiry, each leg's chain IV, the chain's ATM IV as the fallback, the expiry value when there is no IV at all) — a buy-back floored at a nickel, a sale one tick under — re-priced each tick; everything else is HELD and managed to 15:50, then takes the ladder; EVERYTHING crosses at 15:55. OTV4TEST r105's "a credit never crosses; nickel or assignment" is SUPERSEDED. The hard-close page fires only for a position still open past the cross. 📊 GATE tests/check_eod_schedule.py E1-E13, BORN RED on 34b5fff at all thirteen; twenty mutants each caught. 🔴 FOUND BY SWEEPING AFTER THE CLOSE: the first cut read the wall clock around the two seams exit checkers pin (exit_engine.is_hard_close_time, exit_engine.datetime), so eleven checkers were GREEN before 15:45 ET and RED after it — `_eod_due` now goes through both, and E12 pins each seam separately (a mutant survived the first cut of E12, which froze both clocks at once). 🔴 AND A CLASSIFIER GAP: has_assignment_risk read the strategy STRING (of() fails closed to DIRECTIONAL), so a short leg under an unknown strategy would have laddered at 15:50 — it now FAILS TOWARD RISK on the structural columns (short_symbol, center_symbol, is_condor_leg); E3 pins it. FULL SWEEP 180 checkers AFTER THE CLOSE (16:0x ET), base vs build: only check_eod_schedule differs (new); check_land_tooling T3b red on both (worktree-only); live trades.db and resting_orders.db byte-identical and no fixture symbol in the live derived store (its md5 is not a leak test — the live bot's derived engines write it every minute). 16 checkers RE-POINTED by the ruling, each with a changelog naming it (admission A/A8, orb_window W1, volt_plan V1/V3, entry_windows W6, cascade harness copies, one_window_table W5's 18 pins, ladder_wired L7/L8b/L8c, late_credit_window W4/W7, manage_call M3/M4, orb_plan P7/P16b, orb_reentry R1c, orb_restart C11, plan_prepares R8/R14, runaway_plan R9) condor_stop_suppression (its clock pin moved to the is_hard_close_time seam), and one text-search artifact fixed (standing_offer S7 now reads main_loop only; mutant-checked). 📡 BROKER LIQUIDATION (operator asked 2026-09-26 whether the resting orders prevent the broker closing short legs): they lower the odds, cannot guarantee it — the broker's risk desk decides. The live intraday reconcile already finds a short gone with its long still held (closes the record `short_closed_by_broker`, ADOPTS the surviving long, pages); its wind-down sweeps now FOLLOW EOD_SCHEDULE — 15:45, 15:50, 15:55 (NEW: before the cross, in the same tick, so a short liquidated after 15:50 is adopted before the cross sends a two-leg order against a missing leg) and 15:57 — operator: "Agree"; E13 pins it. OPERATOR'S ACTION, NOT CODE: tastytrade lets the account notify their risk desk that late-held positions are actively managed; the timing and basis of their liquidations are still unmeasured. 📄 PAPER, RULED 2026-09-26: *"On paper exits, use latest possible close allowed by code. Ie, the 'end of the ladder' when we walk it (worst-case) or best-case available when deep ITM (par, nickel)"* and *"I want to be accustomed to seeing late closing orders"*: EVERY paper end-of-day close books at the 15:55 cross, at the mark (the ladder's last rung), or at the nickel / parity less a tick when its outcome is already settled (with IV, the 15:55 estimate prices to the same tick as the expiry value; without IV nothing counts as settled). Other paper exits unchanged — their walk already ends at the mark. E7 pins it; four more mutants caught (sixteen in all). | **LAND.1** | ⬜ **A GENUINE RE-ISSUE CANNOT PASS THE LAND GATE.** §26 mandates that re-issuing a revision that ALREADY SHIPPED suffixes it — `r185_r2`, `r185_r3`. `tools/check_land_discipline.py` finds the ledger row with `^\|\s*\*\*(r\d+[a-z]?)\*\*\s*\|`, which matches `OTV4TEST r18` and `r18a` and **cannot match `r18_r2`**. So the gate that enforces §35 refuses the exact revision id §26 requires: the lander appends the row, the checker reports *"no row for r18_r2 — a revision absent from the ledger did not happen"*, and the land dies with the payload loose in the tree. **Hit for real on 2026-09-13** while landing this revision. ⚠️ **IT FAILS CLOSED, WHICH IS THE SAFE DIRECTION** — and that is why nobody has found it: the `_rN` path has simply never been exercised, so a rule this repo has carried since 2026-08-19 has never once been landable. Not urgent here, because this revision was a re-cut of an UNLANDED archive and therefore keeps its own number (the OTV4TEST r12 precedent) — but a real post-land correction will hit it head-on. One character in the regex; the fix needs a born-red fixture with an `_r2` row. (state before: 🔲 OPEN) | ✅ CLOSED r30 — check_land_discipline accepts r<N>_r<M>; check_land_reissue R1-R4. |

For a STATIC pool those are the same number and the code is right; **for a moving tine they differ by slope x elapsed bars**, and the defect arrived with otv4 r163 when tines became pools and nothing revisited the pierce arithmetic. 📊 **SIZING IT:** lookback is `TOUCH_LOOKBACK_BARS = 30` 1m bars; a 1h slope of ~2.0 pts/bar (**what AMD actually carried 09-10/09-11**) is 2.0/60 ≈ 0.033 pts/min, so up to **~1.0 pt of rail drift across the window — 0.20% on a $500 name.** 🔴 **THE GATES IT FEEDS:** `rej >= MIN_REJECTION_PCT` (0.0002) and `rej <= MAX_REJECTION_PCT` (0.0025), so a 0.20% error spans nearly the whole admissible band — and the ceiling exists BECAUSE the measurement matters (shallow <0.10% 33% survived / 0.10-0.25% 34% / 0.25-0.50% 21% / deep >0.50% 19%). **FIX:** measure the extreme against `price_at()` at the TOUCHING bar, the instant the touch was judged at — one line, and it makes the pierce consistent with the touch instead of half time-aware. ⚠️ **TRADING CHANGE (§38.8):** it moves which sweeps clear the gate. **MEASURE FIRST** — how many real tine touches cross a gate boundary when the pierce is computed correctly. 🔑 **SAME SHAPE AS THE ORB STRIKE BRANCH [[LVL.4]] REMOVED THIS MORNING:** a code path written for static levels, silently inherited by a moving one. ✅ **MEASURED, THEN FIXED, THEN RULED — otv4 r377.** 📊 **THE MEASUREMENT FIRST, as the row demanded:** 19,997 distinct 1h fork samples, 15 symbols, 5 sessions. At the full 30-bar lookback the error runs a **median of 10.3% of the admissible band, p90 27.1%**, and **on 57.9% of samples it alone exceeds `MIN_REJECTION_PCT`**. Concentrated rather than diffuse — **AMD's median is 78.1% of the band against QQQ's 1.7%**; this row estimated AMD at 0.20% and it measures 0.18%. 🔴 **AND THE BEHAVIOUR IS WORSE THAN THE ARITHMETIC SUGGESTED.** For a CLEAN touch the extreme sits ON the rail, so the reported depth was `|slope| ✅ **CLOSED otv4 r500** — the work shipped; the status was left half-open. x bars_since` — **the STALENESS of the touch, not its depth.** A graze 25 bars back on a fast rail read as a deep test; a real push on the current bar read as nothing. **The gate preferred stale touches over fresh ones, and preferred them more the faster the rail moved** — which is also why drift alone cleared the floor on most forks. ⚠️ **THE PER-EVENT COUNT COULD NOT BE COMPUTED AND THAT IS WHY THE INSTRUMENTATION SHIPS WITH THE FIX:** the touching BAR has never been recorded — `bar_index` and `reclaim_bar_index` live on the sweep object and neither reaches a plan row — so the bound above is a bound, not a count. `rejection_pct_legacy` now rides the sweep and `pierce_legacy`/`pierce_delta` ride the plan row, record-only, so one session answers it. otv4 r351's pattern. 🔑 **OPERATOR'S RULING, WHICH THE HONEST MEASUREMENT MADE POSSIBLE:** *"broken and reclaimed vs respected are both legitimate setups, both leading to containment inside the channel, so we want both, at the moment it happened, not measured later on a drifted object. It should record the interaction on THAT tick & say which one it was, a graze or a reclaim."* **THE FLOOR BECOMES A BOUNDARY:** below MIN is a **GRAZE**, above is a **RECLAIM**, both fire, and only a BREAK is refused — `pierce_depth` (MAX) still does that for both. The narration names which. ⚠️ **TINES ONLY** — a pool sweep still requires a rejection, because selling into a level price is still through is §36-foundational and otv4 r163's *touch, not a reject* was said about moving tines. **GATES:** new `check_touch_pierce` P1-P5, born red at `720c3f8` on P1/P3/P4 with **P2 (a static pool is unaffected) green both ways as the control** — P1 shows a real 9-point piercing touch reading `0.000000` under the old arithmetic. `check_plan_prepares` v1.11: **T4 RE-DERIVED** — its tape reaches the rail exactly where it stood, so the honest depth is zero, and the check had been asserting a TAKE that existed only because a graze was inflated into a rejection (the otv4 r234 shape, a fixture certifying its own defect) — and **T4b ADDED**, the control that a POOL sweep with a sub-floor rejection is still refused, which **nothing pinned before**, so silently dropping the floor for every sweep would have gone green. Regressions: full sweep at HEAD and on the build, red set IDENTICAL at four, all pre-existing.) | **RPL.4** | 🔴 **OTV4TEST r62'S ORIENTATION REWIRE INVERTED EVERY CREDIT VERTICAL, AND OTV4TEST r62'S LEDGER ROW CLAIMS IT AS A FIX. THIS CORRECTS THE RECORD.** `legs_of` assigns `short_symbol` **−1** and `long_symbol` **+1**, so a credit vertical's combo is `long − short` = **−(spread value)** — which RISES as the spread cheapens, i.e. as the seller wins. **The multi-leg signs already encode favourable orientation.** OTV4TEST r62 applied `flip = -1 if _is_credit(row)` on top and inverted it. **MEASURED, one variable, the book's only replayed credit row:** `pnl_usd −56.0` against a replayed **+28.0** — the book lost, the replay profited. After OTV4TEST r64: **−28.0**, right sign. The nine other replayed rows are byte-identical either way. ⚠️ **AND IT WAS ACCIDENTALLY CORRECT BEFORE OTV4TEST r62.** `is_short_position` has no writer (MEAS.2), so `flip` was `+1` for every row the tool had ever seen — exactly what the spread columns need. **OTV4TEST r62 replaced a dead flag that was right by accident with a live resolver that was wrong on purpose**, and its ledger row records deriving orientation from real columns as a repair. It repaired nothing and introduced this. 🔑 **SURFACED BY THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT, WHICH HAD THE IDENTICAL REWIRE STAGED AND UNLANDED** — blocked by a permission classifier rather than by diligence, as it said plainly. Reproduced here before it was believed. **THE FIX:** the flip is scoped to the single-leg `option_symbol` path, the one place orientation is not already encoded. On this tree **no path we actually trade needs a flip at all** — credit vertical, butterfly `1/−2/1` and single-leg debit are all already favourable-positive; only a naked short would need it, and `OptionsSignal.is_valid` fails closed on one. Kept **guarded and dead** rather than deleted so nobody re-derives why it is absent, with the reachability claim written at the site because *"cannot fire today"* is exactly what silently stops being true. ⚠️ **OTV4TEST r62's SELFTEST ENCODED THE DEFECT AS A REQUIREMENT** — it asserted a credit and a debit vertical with the SAME legs must differ, which is only true while the wrong flip is applied. **Rewritten to the contract that replaced it rather than loosened** (the OTV4TEST r33 precedent). 🔴 **AND OUR CONTROL IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THIS CLASS.** Not one credit row is eligible: our sweeps exit on `sweep_breach_accepted`, which is not a premium stop, so the control correctly excludes every one. It applies to **2 of 10 replayed rows and both are debits**. **A control that can only test premium-stop exits can never say anything about the sweep or the TCS, whose exits are entirely structural.** The control agent measures the same shape at 68% NOT APPLICABLE on its own book. The naming is the gain; the silence remains. **No answer tonight and none invented — this is the limitation, recorded as one.** **GATE:** `check_exit_replay` X11–X13, **born red 2 of 13** at 9116102 with the inverted signs printed verbatim. **X11 asserts PER LEG BY SYMBOL, never a sorted sign list** — `[-1, 1]` is the same list whichever leg carries which, which is why the control agent's equivalent check stayed GREEN on the restored defect. **X13 DRIVES the guarded branch with a synthetic single-leg credit row**, because a mutation disabling its marker changed nothing observable and a gate resting on real rows stayed green on it (measured). Three mutations red against a baseline confirmed green first. | ✅ OTV4TEST r64 |

| **LVL.3** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r18) **THE ACCEPTED FACT COULD NOT SAY WHICH BAR MADE IT.** `derived/levels.py` passed the literal string `"5m"` as `bar_ts` at the ACCEPTED emit site, while the WICKED and REJECTED sites both passed the closed bar's timestamp. **MEASURED ON THIS BOX: all 10 `ACCEPTED` rows carried `bar_ts='5m'`; all 76 `WICKED`/`REJECTED` rows carried a bar.** Two silent consequences. (1) `level_event`'s PK is `(symbol, level_id, bar_ts, event)` and `insert_level_event` is `INSERT OR IGNORE`, so a SECOND acceptance of the same level was **dropped with no error** — the ledger under-reported and the under-report was invisible. (2) `tcs_plan.py` keys its once-per-event latch on `(level_id, bar_ts)`; with `bar_ts` constant that degenerated from ONCE PER ACCEPTANCE to ONCE PER LEVEL for the life of the process. `sweep_plan.py` uses the identical idiom on `REJECTED` and was always correct — only the TCS's upstream fact was stamped with a constant. ⚠️ **FRESHNESS WAS NEVER AFFECTED** (`accepted_age_bars` reads `ts_epoch`), which is exactly why nothing looked wrong. Fixed: the 5m bar's own timestamp, the bar acceptance is judged on. `check_level_rejection` L13/L13b/L13c, born red at OTV4TEST r17 — **L13c measures it: 1 row at OTV4TEST r17, 2 with the fix.** FORWARD-ONLY: the 10 existing rows keep `'5m'`. | ✅ OTV4TEST r18 |
| **LVL.4** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r18) **THE LEVEL ACCOUNTING RAN PER TICK, NOT PER BAR — THREE DEFECTS, THREE RULINGS.** Found by checking the predecessor's 2026-09-12 levels write-up against this tree; all three proven by DRIVING the engine, not by reading. **(1) A TOUCH WAS A POLL.** `derive()` runs every tick and the touch/acceptance block had no bar guard at all: one 5m bar polled 20 times scored `touch_count = 20`. On this box's own ledger `QQQ:PDH (R1):717.52` logged **399 touches in a 390-bar session**, and **566 of 607 levels (93%) logged zero** — the predecessor's AMZN-389 and its 68%/19% split, reproduced on QQQ. **(2) `ACCEPT_CLOSES = 2` COUNTED TICKS.** The same 5m close was re-read every tick, so the SECOND TICK always satisfied it — **~15 seconds and ONE close, not the two the constant names.** **(3) THE RUN NEVER RESET ON AN INSIDE CLOSE.** Only a *touch* (within `TOUCH_TOL_PCT`) cleared `beyond`, so two excursions **90 minutes apart**, with price six points inside for an hour in between, read as two consecutive closes and **retired the level** — pulling it off the board OTV4TEST r15 had only just repaired. ⚠️ **THIS WAS NEVER COSMETIC:** the runaway arms on ACCEPTED (§30) and the TCS triggers on it (§34), so two of the six trades were firing on a word that did not mean what it said. **OPERATOR'S RULINGS, 2026-09-13:** a touch is one per closed bar; two 5m bars is *"Hell no — 10 minutes leaves us nothing actionable, the move is already long over"*; a close back inside breaks the run. Built: the accounting is gated on the **CLOSED 1m bar** — the same bar the rejection fact already used, so the engine is on one clock — making `ACCEPT_CLOSES = 2` mean **two minutes**, deterministic rather than accidental. `check_level_rejection` v1.4 R1/R1b/R2/R2b/R2c/R3/R3b, **seven born red at OTV4TEST r17**, with R1b and R3b as positive controls green on both sides. | ✅ OTV4TEST r18 |

| **LVL.13** | 🔲 **A CLUSTER OF EXTREMES IS ONE ZONE, AND ONLY ITS OUTER MEMBERS DECLARE ANYTHING — THE OPERATOR'S SPEC, 2026-09-17.** **The finding:** measured against his own 1D chart, the live board carried **716.75, 716.85, 717.05, 717.52, 717.68** — five rungs inside **0.93** — before reaching anything he would call a level, so "3 above" resolved to a cluster instead of 717.68 / 719.70 / 721.85. **THE SPEC, in his words:** *"When session extremes, by recency first, cluster in close proximity I want the extremes of the outermost (highest, lowest) of the cluster to count as a touch when the nearest part of the zone is tested, and breached when the furthest part of the cluster is accepted beyond"*; *"it's just grouping and only the outer members of the group declare anything"*; *"when we have a breach of the cluster everything that was a part of the cluster needs to go with it."* **The rules, in full:** (1) A zone has exactly **TWO recording prices** — its outermost high and its outermost low. (2) A test of the **NEAR** edge registers the touch. (3) Acceptance beyond the **FAR** edge is the breach, and **retires the ENTIRE cluster, every member with it** — not only the edge accepted through. (4) Acceptance beyond the near edge alone declares **nothing**. (5) Every member **between** the edges is **SILENT**: no WICKED, no REJECTED, no ACCEPTED, no touch count. They are the zone's biography, not its voice. (6) ⚠️ **IT IS A GROUPING, NOT A NEW STORE AND NOT A NEW MAP.** The ledger goes on recording session extremes exactly as it does now; the ONE board (OTV4TEST r33) decides which two are the live edges and which are absorbed. 🔑 **AND IT DISSOLVES A PROBLEM RATHER THAN CREATING ONE:** `touch_count` does not have to migrate from the member to the zone, because under rule 5 the members never counted anything. **THE WIDTH IS A PRIOR AND §31 GOVERNS — IT IS NOT YET SET.** His framing: *"an area defined by the region several 1-day wicks occupy in a concurrent time frame — measured by extremes, but often characterized by the presence of wicks."* Measured locally on **28** QQQ daily bars (a mechanism, not a conclusion — §12): upper wick median 1.69 / p75 2.81, lower 2.12 / 2.71, **all wicks median 1.91, p75 2.71, p90 3.79** — 0.27% / 0.38% / 0.53% of spot, against a median daily range of 6.26 (a typical wick ≈ **30% of the day's range**). 🔑 **THE ANSWER LOOKS ROBUST RATHER THAN FITTED**, because the live gaps are **bimodal**: six gaps at or under 0.47 and four at or above 1.16, with **nothing in between**. Median and p75 therefore give the IDENTICAL grouping — **5 zones from 11 levels, two above spot and three below**; only p90 (3.79) over-merges, swallowing 720.06 and 716.75 into one 3.31-wide zone, which are two different trades. **OWED BEFORE IT BECOMES A NUMBER:** the same calculation on the S3 daily history (LVL.12's pull — one job, both answers) across more than one symbol; expressed as a **fraction of daily range or ATR rather than a fixed dollar amount**, or it breaks on the first volatility change and on every symbol but QQQ; and a **born-red gate on the asymmetry** — near edge counts the touch, far edge takes the breach and takes the whole cluster with it, interior declares nothing. **§36 CATEGORY: FOUNDATIONAL** — it changes what a level IS, which is the class §38.3 reserves to the operator. **SEQUENCED BEHIND LVL.12 by his ruling: "wait till the history is in."** ✅ **CLOSED OTV4TEST r39 — ALL THREE OWED ITEMS PAID.** (a) **More than one symbol, and the answer changed shape because of it:** the width is NOT a fraction of spot and NOT a fixed dollar amount — it is the **median larger-side HOURLY wick, recomputed every board**. Measured across QQQ, MU and SPX, the wick holds at **31-37% of the median hourly range** while the same wick **as a percent of spot spans four-fold**. | **ADM.2** | ✅ **(OTV4TEST r50) DISSOLVED, NOT DECIDED — THE OPERATOR RULED OUT THE BLOCKING PARADIGM ITSELF.** 2026-09-18: *"In normal (live) trading, one would assume each trade would gate another. I don't like that paradigm. I envision every trade to be 'right' in its own time, as long as our entries are weighing the right information… two separate trades, even potentially trading opposite directions, may have been right at the time that they fired… If we write our plans properly and we write our strategies properly, a given set of conditions for entry may be right at a particular time and I don't see any reason to stop it from firing if it cleared the bar, even if the thesis is the opposite of an existing open trade."* 🔑 **THE COLLISION BECOMES UNREACHABLE RATHER THAN ADJUDICATED.** With `blocked_by` empty permanently, a grant can never contradict a block, so none of the three candidate rulings below needs taking. **OTV4TEST r35's named sets stay in place, config-toggleable and simply never armed** — which is what a named-set design is for, and why this reversal costs nothing. 🔑 **AND THE GRANT CHANGES MEANING, FOR THE BETTER.** Under hierarchy a grant was PERMISSION — an override of a block. With nothing to override it becomes **INFORMATION**: *I evaluated this break and concluded it is a harvest; the fade is yours if you want it.* The receiver still clears its own bar. That is strictly more useful than an override and is closer to what BRK.1 wants from Breakout→hunt (OTV4TEST r48). ⚠️ **WHAT DOES NOT DISAPPEAR — IT MOVES FROM PERMISSION TO CAPITAL.** His argument is about CORRECTNESS and it holds: two setups can both be right at the moment they fire. But the constraint hierarchy was accidentally supplying was **EXPOSURE**, not correctness. Four strategies at full risk-per-trade is 4x one, and OTV4TEST r44 gave the ORB a full-budget slice where it previously took 3%. **The catastrophic cap is now the ONLY thing between 'everything cleared its bar' and a very large day** — which is answerable with a number rather than a hierarchy, and is his to set. 🔑 **AND IT IS SMALLER THAN THE ARITHMETIC SUGGESTS:** two opposite-direction trades on one underlying are partly a HEDGE — gross risk 2x, net directional exposure much less. ⚠️ **WHAT STILL NEEDS COORDINATING, AND IS NOT HIERARCHY: `max_open_of_type`** (sweep 2, everything else 1). That is not one trade gating another, it is a strategy declining to stack on ITSELF, which his reasoning does not cover and which stays. **THE ORIGINAL QUESTION, KEPT FOR THE RECORD:** Operator, 2026-09-18: *"it's gonna be non-competing for the next couple of weeks, but after that we will be going back to a hierarchy based and there will be some blocking between the trades."* 🔑 **THE TABLE IS ALREADY BUILT FOR THAT HALF** — OTV4TEST r35 made `blocks` and `blocked_by` NAMED SETS, empty by his ruling, overlaid from `config.ADMISSION_RULES`, so re-introducing blocking is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. That was the whole point of the design. ⚠️ **WHAT IS NOT SETTLED IS THE COLLISION.** `execution/handoff.py` was written when the slot rule was binary: a live grant made *"the slot rule yield for that strategy only"*, and **the slot rule no longer exists** (OTV4TEST r43). When `blocked_by` comes back, a grant and a block can name the same pair and DISAGREE — and that is not hypothetical, because BRK.1 has Breakout DEFERRING TO THE HUNT BY GRANT (OTV4TEST r48) while hierarchy would plausibly have one block the other. **Three candidate rulings, all defensible, and it is the operator's call: (a) the grant WINS — an explicit token from the blocking strategy is consent, and blocking is only the default; (b) the block WINS — a grant answers the slot question only, which is what its own docstring says, and the slot question is gone; (c) the grant wins ONLY when `from_strategy` is the blocker itself**, so A-blocks-B plus A-grants-to-B admits B, while C's grant cannot override A's block. **(c) is the narrowest and matches the grant's original intent most closely.** ⚠️ **DECIDE IT WHILE IT IS CHEAP.** Today both mechanisms are inert — blocks are empty and the only grant path is hunt→sweep — so the collision is unreachable and costs nothing to settle. On the first hierarchy session it becomes a live ordering question with money on it, and the failure mode is silent: the deferred-to strategy simply never fires and nothing says why. **OWED: a born-red checker covering all three pairs (grant only, block only, both) whichever ruling is taken.** | ✅ OTV4TEST r50 |
| **ADM.1** | ✅ **(OTV4TEST r43) THE OPEN-BRANCH BLOCK IS THE LAST OF OTV4TEST r35's SEVEN MECHANISMS STILL DECIDING WHAT MAY OPEN.** 🔑 **OPERATOR'S RULING 2026-09-18, WHICH SETTLES IT AND WIDENS IT:** *"I want the orb, hunt, breakout & sweep all able to fire & non-competing. We only have one session at a time so we need to maximize every opportunity on these setups. In live trading, we will revert back to hierarchy-based."* ⚠️ **THE POSTURE IS TEST-BOX-ONLY AND THAT IS THE POINT** — non-compete exists to harvest the maximum number of comparable setups from ONE session, not because hierarchy is wrong. The fleet reverts to hierarchy, and because the whole table overlays from `config.ADMISSION_RULES` (OTV4TEST r35), that reversion is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. 🔴 **AND IT IS BIGGER THAN REMOVING A GATE: `attempt_new_entry`'s `signal` CASCADE *IS* THE COMPETITION** — one variable, five `if signal is None` guards, winner-take-all, so the first strategy to fire consumes the tick. Non-competing means each ADMITTED strategy is asked and executed independently. 🔑 **THE PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS AND IS PROVEN IN PRODUCTION:** `_attempt_hunt` (OTV4TEST r12) asks its own plan and executes ADDITIVE, taking no slot — *"the hunt runs beside the ORB, never in its slot"*. The conversion is to that template, not to something new. ⚠️ **AND IT DELETES HAND-ROLLED CAPS:** `_attempt_hunt` carries its own *"a hunt is already open on this box"* check, which is `max_open_of_type: 1` doing the same job twice — two mechanisms for one rule is how they drift. ⚠️ **CONCURRENCY CEILING RISES:** worst case four directional debits in one session at full risk-per-trade, with sweep's cap at 2, and Breakout makes five entry types. | `main.py`'s tick loop still reads `if not pos_mgr.has_blocking_position(): attempt_new_entry(...)`, so a box holding an ORB or runaway debit refuses every credit entry — **against the operator's ruling of 2026-09-17**: *"to the greatest extent possible I do not want to set a maximum number of open positions… I WANT sweep to be able to fire while we have an active runaway in progress. We will rely on the runaway to exit gracefully using ITS OWN stops."* The admission table's per-type caps (sweep 2, everything else 1) are built to replace it and `blocks`/`blocked_by` are named sets, empty by his ruling. 🔴 **WHAT BLOCKS THE DELETION, MEASURED AT OTV4TEST r40 RATHER THAN GUESSED:** removing the gate orphans `_attempt_butterfly` and `_attempt_sweep_on_grant`, whose only callers are the three direct calls in that same branch — and those are **not** duplicates of `attempt_new_entry`'s own dispatch. `_attempt_butterfly(additive=True)` is OTV4TEST r161's EXEMPT path, asked every tick of its slot with a fire APPENDED to whatever is open; `attempt_new_entry`'s butterfly sits behind `elif signal is None:` and competes for the slot. Running both asks three strategies TWICE A TICK; deleting the three loses the exemption that cost MU, NFLX and TSLA a full credit session on 2026-08-31 (OTV4TEST r197). ⚠️ **THE `additive` ARGUMENT IS ALREADY A NO-OP IN THAT BRANCH** — OTV4TEST r197 made it a fact (`if additive or _pm.has_open_position()`) — so the de-duplication is tractable; it is the ASK-ORDERING that needs deciding, not the record-keeping. **§36 CATEGORY: FOUNDATIONAL** — it changes what may be open simultaneously, which §38.3 reserves to the operator. **NOT ATTEMPTED UNATTENDED:** found at 23:05 on a box that wakes into a trading session at 08:00. | ✅ OTV4TEST r43 |

| **BOX.12** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r70) **/tmp IS tmpfs — IT IS RAM — AND SESSION SCRATCHPADS ACCUMULATE UNTIL EVERY SHELL DIES SILENTLY.** Measured: `/tmp` here is a **455 MB tmpfs of 908 MB physical**, and the agent scratchpad is keyed by SESSION ID, so every new session mints a directory and nothing removes the old ones. One of mine reached **108 MB in three git clones** — RAM the bot could not have, and part of why a corpus study was OOM-reaped. 🔴 **WHAT IT COSTS IS THE SHELL, NOT DISK.** When tmpfs fills, every Bash spawn needs a temp file for its shell snapshot and dies BEFORE executing: instant exit 1, no stdout, no stderr, while Read/Write keep working because they never spawn. **The control box hit exactly this — 1.5 GB across 57,304 files, `df` reading 384 MB free throughout because the binding limit was a `usrquota`, not free space.** An agent that looks brain-dead rather than out of space, with nothing logged anywhere (§0.5). ⚠️ **THIS BOX WAS PROTECTED BY ACCIDENT, NOT DESIGN** — tmpfs is volatile and the midnight halt plus the 08:00 wake clears it daily. Control does not reboot daily. **BUILT:** `_claude_purge_scratch` in devtools (HAND OFF, RESUME and the picker) and `purge_scratch()` in `tools/claude_boot.py`. **ORDER IS THE OPERATOR'S RULING — PURGE, THEN KILL, THEN LAUNCH** — not cosmetic: if tmpfs is already full the tmux kill may not spawn either. 🔑 **IT ARCHIVES, IT NEVER DELETES** (`~/claude_scratch_archive`, 14-day sweep the only deleting path) — the mainline control agent's finding, from a near-miss where a deleting purge would have destroyed an entire unlanded revision sitting in a handed-off session's scratchpad. **The harness itself instructs every agent to work in the scratchpad**, so treating it as disposable is wrong by construction. 🔴 **AND THEIR REVIEW CAUGHT A LIVE DEFECT IN MY FIRST CUT:** the purge ran UNCONDITIONALLY while `bring_up()` returns "already running" WITHOUT killing anything — and under systemd `CLAUDE_SCRATCH` is unset, so nothing was protected. **`systemctl start optbot-claude-boot.service` against a LIVE session would have archived the running agent's working directory out from under it**, and the operator ran exactly that command on 2026-09-20. Gated on `agent_alive()` now: **only reclaim when you are replacing.** Free-tmpfs rides the boot status string, so it reaches the boot Telegram beside the IP with no change to `alert_manager`. GATE `tests/check_claude_purge.py` P0–P10, **born red 10 of 11**; P7 control green both sides. | ✅ CLOSED |

| **GATE.7** | ✅ **CLOSED r9** — FOUR CHECKERS WERE RED BEFORE 09:30 ET BY THE CLOCK ALONE. CD.2 bounds the trend and sweep plans' level reads by today's session open with no pre-open fallback (`tcs_plan` / `sweep_plan`: `session_open_epoch or _session_open_epoch()`), and the checkers pin `session_open_epoch` so they do not depend on the clock - but they handed the pin to the STRATEGY wrappers (`TrendCreditSpread`, `SweepCreditSpreadStrategy`), whose `prepare` / `generate_signal` swallowed it in `**_ignored`. Before 09:30 ET the bound was a future epoch and every fixture level was invisible: check_levels_in_play, check_plan_prepares, check_tcs_plan and check_tcs_fifty read red on every 08:00 ET boot sweep (and on the operator's first install sweep) and green after the open. Found, bisected and root-caused by QQQ-TEST on a store-less sweep of r8 (MSG-QQQ-1025-04). Both wrappers now take `session_open_epoch` (default 0.0) and forward it; the live path passes none, so it reads today's 09:30 ET exactly as before (no behaviour change). No trading change: both plans are off by default and dormant (TRADES §0) and hour-gated after the open. Every fixture call of either wrapper now carries a pin: check_tcs_plan T11 and check_plan_prepares T4-T7 (which the pin decides), and check_entry_windows, check_tcs_narrates and check_tcs_parked (paths that stop before any level read); every expectation unchanged. GATE `check_session_pin`: P1-P4 spy on plan.prepare (forwarding, red or green by the code at any hour), P5 the no-pin value is exactly 0.0, P6 walks tests/check_*.py and requires `session_open_epoch` on every call of a wrapper, so the next unpinned fixture is caught in the afternoon too (QQQ-TEST's critique, MSG-QQQ-1025-05). BORN RED on r8 at P1-P6 (P6: eight unpinned sites); GREEN after. The defect is red only while the wall clock is before 09:30 ET: run at 08:27 ET, the four checkers are RED on r8 and GREEN on r9. | ✅ CLOSED r9 |
